# MMBCC Decomp — Core Memory & Handoff

**Dedicated memory file for the Mega Man Battle Chip Challenge (USA, GBA)
matching decompilation. Every session, coordinator, and worker reads this
first and logs here.** Lives in the project tree (`~/workspace/mmbcc-decomp/`)
so it survives VM churn with the home directory and rides future GitHub
pushes. This file does NOT replace `PROGRESS.md` (wave log),
`docs/match_status.tsv` (registry), or `~/MEMORY.md` (user-level memory) —
it is the decomp's own working memory: state, handoff, method, rulings, and
lessons, in one place.

---

## User directives (standing)

- **Byte-for-byte 1:1 rule (2026-10-20):** done only when `make compare`
  passes — built ROM byte-identical to the baserom. Near-matches never
  count.
- **Reporting rule (2026-10-25, superseding 2026-10-23):** next update at
  70% (crossed 2026-10-26), then only at +5% intervals from 70% (75%
  crossed in Wave 66; next 80%), plus the once-daily 9 AM summary. Every
  update carries a progress bar with percentage. No per-wave chat updates.
- **Manual method directive (2026-10-30):** decompile the remaining
  functions BY HAND, manually — hand-written C from disassembly and
  semantic reasoning, not blind trial loops. Analyze GBA emulator sources
  as reference for resolving non-matching functions: mGBA and VBA-M
  (see Emulator reference below). Log everything here.
- **GitHub:** current progress pushed 2026-10-29 (2 commits, b99a2a65 +
  0aeaa01f = HEAD, Petari-style README). This file rides the next push.
- **Recomp feed:** decomp findings feed `docs/feeds_recomp_notes.md` for
  the recomp track.

  the two case-40 pool literals at positions 1053/1061) — is NOT invented

  27764's case-0 dance (N5 + M1 + E6-form case 1 combination) has only one

  is a different instruction, not a load, and is arguably outside the
  sanctioned list as written. Worker C did NOT try it. Report to the user
  in the next daily summary in plain language; no wave attempts it until
  the user rules.

  #1 in the 2026-11-06 daily summary. Scope: the inline-asm `lsls; lsrs`
  pair for 27764's case-0 dance ONLY — the same class as the sanctioned

  no new logic). Wave 357's Worker C may now try it on the AC2/AC4 base
  (57/57). Land ONLY a form verified 0-diff against its TRUE extent,

  **OPEN RULING QUESTION (user, pending — do NOT attempt any
  scheduling-level intervention without a ruling):** 3CA7C's remaining
  case7 gaps (idx computed in r1 with the `cmp r3, #5; bne; subs r1, #1`
  guard vs ROM's branch-internal `subs r0; lsls r0; lsrs r1, r0`; the mode
  preset at D810 vs ROM's D80A; the 0x46A4 offset loaded into r1 vs ROM's
  r2) have resisted every sanctioned source-shape trial across Waves
  341–342; the decision-shape itself may be compiler-scheduling-bound
  (the preset position and the r0→r1 shift pair are both
  instruction-scheduling artifacts of the original compiler, not
  source-shape artifacts). The only forms that DO reach the ROM shape
  (v73's `subs r0`, v76's single test, v81's r2 offset) each destroy the
  allocation/layout around it — or miscompile (v81). If the gaps are
  scheduling-bound, further C-only trials will keep regressing and the
  function may need a ruling on a different class of steering (e.g.
  whether a scheduling-level intervention is sanctioned, or whether the
  remaining ~630 case7 diffs are acceptable as a non-matching residual).
  Report to the user in the next daily summary in plain language; no wave
  attempts any scheduling-level intervention until the user rules.
  **PARENT DECISION 2026-11-06 — 27764 EXPLICIT C SHIFT EXPRESSION IS IN
  SCOPE:** Wave 350's Worker C flagged an untried candidate — an explicit
  C shift expression, e.g. `h = (gg << 16) >> 16;` with h pinned to r1 —
  as possibly outside the three sanctioned forms (pins / inline-asm ldrsb
  / alias), and did not build it. RULED IN SCOPE: a register pin is an
  explicitly sanctioned form, and the shift expression is ordinary C source
  that the compiler itself lowers — it computes exactly the value ROM
  computes (zero-extend of the low 16 bits), invents no logic, adds no
  branch, changes no semantics. It is compiler steering in the same class
  as everything already sanctioned under the 2026-11-05 ruling. Wave 351's
  C may try it (and the int-copy-pinned-to-r0 variant). This is distinct
  from the still-forbidden inline-asm `lsls`/`lsrs` pair, which hand-emits
  an instruction rather than letting the compiler lower ordinary C — that
  remains pending the user's ruling. The 2026-11-06 shift-expression
  decision is DISCHARGED (Wave 351): the explicit shift does NOT
  materialise an r0 intermediate — `(gg<<16)>>16` on an int lowers to
  lsl+asr while the conversion lowers to lsl+lsr, a double shift pair (+8
  B, built 1228); the explicit-shift dimension is CLOSED in all four
  pin/width combinations (r0/r1/unpinned × int/u16).
  **COORDINATOR CLASSIFICATION 2026-11-06 (parent-endorsed; the user
  ruling itself is unchanged) — ldrsb-for-u8-load substitution is IN SCOPE
  as a trial under the 2026-11-05 ruling:** Worker E substituted inline-asm
  ldrsb for a u8 load in the 40CC4 NEG with a stored-byte equivalence proof
  (only the low byte is stored, so sign-extension is unobservable in the
  NEG) — behavior-preserving under the stated proof, same class as a
  semantic correction proven from ROM disassembly/census. Boundary, as
  drawn by E: ldrsb for `_win2` was rejected as unsafe (the byte feeds
  comparisons and gp[21]) and was not built. All three ldrsb-NEG trials
  were harmful; any future landing on this basis still requires a verified
  0-diff plus a comment citing the proof.
  **OPEN RULING QUESTION (Worker E, flagged Wave 352 — do NOT try without
  a ruling):** factor the shared GAUGE emit tail (`gp[22] &= 0xFFFC3F;
  Sub_0800800C(G_02004D14, 0);`) into a `static` helper called from the
  case-40/80 emits, to mimic ROM's shared tail at 0x4130e (ROM's case-40/80
  GAUGE emits branch to the shared tail while 200/100 inline it; the built
  code inlines the tail in all four cases and the tails cannot
  cross-jump-merge because the `ldr r1,[pc,#N]` immediate differs per
  site). It is a source restructure beyond the sanctioned pin/ldrsb/alias
  list; the helper body would sit after the function (outside the 2152 B
  extent, so the size accounting needs judgement); and the call would be
  `bl` (4 B) where ROM has `b` (2 B) — so even in the best case two bytes
  per site would still differ. It is the only identified mechanism for the
  40/80 emit-tail difference. Report to the user in the next daily summary
  in plain language; no wave attempts it until the user rules.

## Durable idioms & lessons (what actually works)

- clobber-reservation; pin-the-thief; volatile-store DSE defeat;
  dis2asm2 label-rebase; try old_agbcc before fighting allocator
  rotation; single-expression `v=(*p&m)|k`; naked-asm for
  compiler-impossible prologues; inline-asm ldrsb (agbcc never emits
  ldrsb/ldrsh from C).
- Pool-barrier mechanism (gcc thumb_reorg dumps the literal pool at the
  first BARRIER after the first constant load — predicts ROM mid/end-pool
  layouts; found by reading thumb.c). Pool-const pin (block-scoped
  register `T v __asm__("rN") = CONST` forces the pool destination).
- Any `register __asm__("r7")` pin — even block-scoped — marks r7 fixed
  function-wide. Do NOT clobber a pinned register.
- Verify against TRUE extent, never a self-measured window (Wave 29's
  "48 B bloat" was a measurement error). Trailing-pool size-table
  harness fix corrected 56 extents and caught a real misdecode.
- Rect-fill graphics-family arc closed via sanctioned asm transcription;
  BlendCopy twins (15-bit color blenders) matched; sanctioned
  transcription is allowed only where the coordinator sanctions it —
  never as a first resort.
- Build catches: `--section-start` alone fails (custom .ld needed); link
  thunks at ROM address; Makefile sed needs `$$` escaping; nm hygiene
  must grep ALL lines per name (never `head -1`); verify line count
  after scripted ldscript edits; staged files need
  `__attribute__((section(...)))` for the real ldscript.
- Factory quirk: header checksum byte @0xBD is `0x7B` vs computed
  `0x94`; the build reproduces `0x7B`.
- Text is a custom Capcom `BE 66 BC 00` container (codec unknown —
  hardest Phase 4 sub-task). Audio is a proprietary Capcom driver
  (stretch goal). Save driver fully mapped (6 primitives at
  0x0804DB38–0x0804DC94; timing-critical code executes from an IWRAM
  stack buffer — must be preserved by the recomp).

## Emulator reference plan (manual method)

Use emulator sources as a reference for GBA HARDWARE SEMANTICS when a
function resists matching — not as code to copy, but to understand what
the game code must be doing:

- **mGBA** (github.com/mgba-emu/mgba): `src/gb/` — BIOS calls, memory
  map, DMA, timers, PPU/video, audio, serial, save types (SRAM/Flash/
  EEPROM), ARM7TDMI core quirks (pipeline, wait states, BIOS math).
- **VBA-M** (github.com/visualboyadvance-m/visualboyadvance-m): same
  areas as a second opinion where mGBA and the ROM disagree.
- Map each hard function to the hardware it touches first (registers,
  DMA channels, BIOS swi calls, save hardware, display timing), then
  hand-write the C to match the observed behavior AND the exact bytes.
  The byte gate never moves: a semantic explanation that doesn't produce
  a 0-diff is not a landing.
- Log every emulator-derived insight here with the function address it
  unlocked, so the reasoning is reusable.

## Evaluated resource — automated-recompilers (user-shared 2026-11-05, NOT adopted)

- **Repo:** https://github.com/yefan911/automated-recompilers — Automated
  Game ROM Recompiler, MIT, v0.1.0-beta, created 2026-10-30, 19 stars,
  48 commits at check time (2026-11-05 23:23 UTC). User sent the link
  with "use this repo if it helps".
- **Verdict: do NOT switch to it, for either track.** It is a static
  AOT recompiler (ROM -> lifted `bb_XXXXXXXX` C -> native .exe), not a
  matching-decomp tool: its output is machine-lifted C, not hand-written
  matching C, so it cannot produce a byte-identical agbcc build and
  cannot help the decomp's byte gate. For the recomp track it is far
  behind the working build: ARM lifter only 69.8% (30/43) and MISSING
  `LDM`/`STM`, `LDRSB`/`LDRSH`, `MRS`/`MSR`, multiply-long — `LDRSB`
  is exactly the idiom the stuck functions turn on; Thumb 89.4%.
  Its HAL is Mode 3 video only — tilemap PPU Modes 0/1/2, DMA audio
  and SRAM/Flash saves are all Milestone 2/3 "in progress / planned",
  not done. Its "commercial verification" is boot-init only (GBA
  example: `PGATour.exe` merely sets `REG_DISPCNT = 0x0080`). The
  existing Windows build (mstan/gbarecomp, user-confirmed working,
  SRAM save + launcher) stays the recomp baseline.
- **Only reusable bit:** `docs/CASE_STUDIES.md` is a readable summary
  of N64Recomp / mstan gbarecomp / JRickey gba-recomp (hybrid AOT +
  fallback interpreter for IWRAM code, scanline cycle-quota sync) —
  reference reading only. Its CFG jump-table heuristic
  (`src/lifter/cfg.py`) may be worth a look only if a coordinator
  hits a switch-table question; it does not produce matching C.
  Do not re-evaluate this repo as a replacement without a new
  version / new evidence.
